Strong and Weak BSD in Applied Simulation
A critical axis of this theory’s viability lies in the distinction and observed behavior of strong versus weak forms of the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer (BSD) conjecture under simulation. These forms guide both the theoretical scaffolding and the validation architecture of the Arithmetic–Cosmic Structure Conjecture.
                                                                                                * The weak BSD asserts the equality between the rank of an elliptic curve and the order of vanishing of its L-function at 𝒔 = 1.

                                                                                                * The strong BSD includes this, but also asserts the exact formula for the leading coefficient of the L-function’s Taylor expansion at 𝒔 = 1, incorporating Tamagawa numbers, the regulator, the order of the torsion subgroup, and the order of the Shafarevich–Tate group \Sha(𝑬).

In simulation, curves that satisfy weak BSD (rank-L correspondence) were prioritized for projection stability. Using SageMath and LMFDB data, a large number of such curves were sampled, and their symbolic entropy and spatial projection tested. These projections consistently aligned with expected curvature zones—e.g., rank 1 curves mapping to filamentary structures.
To test the strong BSD, deeper arithmetic invariants were calculated. For curves with known or conjectured \Sha(𝑬) = 1, the full BSD formula was evaluated numerically. These curves served as canonical samples for high-fidelity projection because every component of their symbolic structure was fully accountable.
In the projection :
                                                                                                   * Strong BSD-holding curves preserved symbolic entropy with minimal deviation under projection.

                                                                                                   * Weak BSD-only curves required regulator-weighted correction but retained topological validity.

Simulations also revealed that failing to satisfy either BSD form resulted in projection artifacts:
                                                                                                      * Elevated symbolic entropy loss.

                                                                                                      * Polar clustering and curvature inconsistency.

                                                                                                      * Disrupted rank–density alignment.

Thus, the BSD conjecture does not merely inspire the theory—it regulates its internal logic. The closer a curve adheres to BSD predictions, the more stable, consistent, and interpretable its projection becomes. In symbolic cosmology, BSD becomes a constraint engine, refining which curves are admitted to the structure-preserving projection.
This computational insight bridges conjecture and cosmos: when elliptic curves conform to BSD conditions, their mappings into space-time structure exhibit coherence with empirical large-scale data. This outcome supports the theory’s central claim—that the arithmetic of elliptic curves holds cosmological relevance when filtered through entropy-preserving projection.

Rank Curvature Mapping
One of the core insights of the Arithmetic–Cosmic Structure Conjecture is the proposed equivalence between algebraic rank and spatial curvature in a cosmological manifold. In this framework, the rank of an elliptic curve over ℚ —a measure of the number of independent rational points—is reinterpreted as a curvature operator that governs the shape, density, and geometric behavior of the region into which the curve is projected.
Symbolically, the mapping is described as:
Rank(𝑬)Curvature((𝑬))
This duality is operationalized through the elevation coordinate 𝐳 in the symbolic projection (𝑬), where:
                                                                                                                                                * 𝐳=, with =rank(𝑬), and is a curvature normalization constant.
                                                                                                                                                * Higher ranks induce greater symbolic elevation, interpreted topologically as sharper curvature or clustering.
Interpretive Consequences:
                                                                                                                                                   * Rank 0 curves project to regions of minimal curvature—cosmic voids or topological basins.


                                                                                                                                                   * Rank 1 curves correspond to filamentary backbones—stable geodesic channels through symbolic space.


                                                                                                                                                   * Rank 2 and 3+ curves project into high-curvature, multi-connected regions like galaxy clusters and nodal intersections.
This mapping is supported by persistent homology evaluations, where Betti number variations in SDSS filament data show statistical alignment with symbolic curvature levels induced by elliptic curve rank.
Moreover, the curvature effect is not merely visual. It governs the symbolic entropy distribution, shapes divergence in the Euler–Lagrange formulation, and constrains the torsion-weighted density of projected features.
In summary, the rank  curvature mapping provides a direct bridge from the discrete arithmetic structure of elliptic curves to the continuous geometric tapestry of the cosmos—elevating rank from an abstract group-theoretic property to a tangible measure of topological form.

Regulator Density Projection
In parallel with the curvature implications of elliptic curve rank, the regulator 𝑹 plays a critical role in modeling cosmic density within the symbolic projection framework. While rank determines the symbolic elevation, regulator encodes the spatial “thickness” or energy intensity of the projection, governing how density flows through the topology of the cosmic manifold.
In number theory, the regulator measures the covolume of the lattice formed by rational points under the Néron–Tate canonical height pairing. Symbolically, this reflects how broadly a curve spreads its information across space. High 𝓡 implies arithmetic dispersion; low 𝑹, compression.
This leads to the proposed mapping:
Regulator(𝑬)Density((𝑬))
Here, density refers not to mere galaxy count, but to the symbolic energy per unit curvature—an entropy-weighted field quantity that governs topological significance.
Interpretive Consequences:
                                                                                                                                                   * Low 𝑹  curves correspond to underdense regions—projecting into symbolic voids or low-entropy troughs.


                                                                                                                                                   * Moderate 𝑹  indicates structural channels—filaments with consistent, organized density.


                                                                                                                                                   * High 𝑹  projections generate concentrated symbolic mass—akin to galaxy clusters or gravitational basins.
In practice, the projection
(𝑬) = (log,log 𝑵,𝐳 =,log 𝑹)
encodes log 𝑹 as a density shaping modifier in the 3-sphere manifold, directly influencing the volume and curvature envelope of the projected region.
This mapping is empirically validated through kernel density estimation and persistent homology across SDSS and GAMA datasets, showing that symbolic entropy correlates with galaxy number density in projection zones derived from high-regulator curves.
Thus, regulator  density becomes a second symbolic pillar in the cosmological embedding—transforming arithmetic structure into measurable spatial energy and completing the entropy–geometry duality alongside the rank–curvature map.

L-function Structure and Phase Transition Interpretation
One of the deepest links between number theory and physical cosmology in this theory arises from the interpretation of the L-function of an elliptic curve as an analog of a phase transition operator in symbolic space.
The L-function 𝑳(𝑬,𝑺), defined for an elliptic curve 𝑬/ℚ, encodes the arithmetic of the curve in a complex variable 𝒔. The behavior of this function near 𝒔 = 1 is particularly important—its order of vanishing defines the analytic rank, and its leading Taylor coefficient appears in the full BSD formula.
In this projection-based cosmological model, the symbolic L-function is reinterpreted as:

where  represents galaxy masses and  is a normalization constant derived from peak mass density. This function acts as a symbolic free energy potential—measuring how symbolic mass is distributed across spatial configurations.
Interpretive Consequences:
                                                                                                                                                   * The critical point 𝒔 = 1 is reinterpreted as a phase boundary between entropy-dominated and structure-dominated regions.


                                                                                                                                                   * Curves with L-functions sharply vanishing at 𝒔 = 1 correspond to symbolic regions undergoing topological phase transitions—e.g., void collapse or filament birth.


                                                                                                                                                   * The growth or decay behavior of for predicts symbolic mass accretion, entropy condensation, or structural decay.
Empirical alignment of symbolic L-functions with smoothed galaxy mass distributions (via KDE and GMM analysis) shows that inflection points in  often coincide with cosmological scale transitions—i.e., from filament to hub, or void to filament.
Symbolically, this means that L-functions do more than summarize arithmetic—they encode energetic structure and curvature change across the projected manifold. Just as statistical mechanics tracks thermodynamic behavior via partition functions, this theory interprets symbolic L-functions as the statistical mechanics of projection—tracking how arithmetic identity manifests in the geometry and entropy of the observable universe.
Thus, L-function behavior becomes a diagnostic of symbolic phase, and phase transitions become events not only of matter aggregation, but of arithmetic reconfiguration.

Criteria for Alternative Mappings Φ
PTD, MCJ, FT, IWT Variants
While the primary mapping  in this theory is inspired by the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer framework, several alternative projection schemes have been developed to test symbolic robustness, eliminate dependency on unproven components of BSD, and capture different facets of arithmetic geometry. These variants provide diverse symbolic embeddings and enable cross-validation of curvature–density projections.
________________


PTD Mapping (Period–Torsion–Discriminant):
 = (log⁡Ω,#E,log⁡∣Δ∣)


This mapping prioritizes period integrals and torsion subgroup structure, offering strong entropy preservation in low-rank curves and excellent alignment with symmetric filament formations.
________________


MCJ Mapping (Modularity–Conductor–j-invariant):
 = (log,𝑹(log𝑗()),𝑰(log𝑗()))
Using the modular parameter space, this embedding reveals elliptic modular surfaces and highlights clustering aligned with phase-space bifurcations. The j-invariant’s complex logarithm allows symbolic compression of modular symmetry.
________________


FT Mapping (Frobenius Trace Spectrum):
 = (,,σ())
This model uses Frobenius traces at various primes 𝒑, capturing local Galois deformation and modularity fluctuation. It is suited to simulate fine-grained structural transitions in low-density cosmic zones.
________________


IWT Mapping (Isogeny–Weighted Torsion):
 = (log⁡deg(ϕ),#,𝑰sogRank(𝑬))
Here, isogeny classes and torsion growth control symbolic mass. The IsogRank metric represents the position of a curve within its isogeny tree—capturing arithmetic symmetry and curvature equivalence under morphism.
________________


Each variant serves as a symbolic probe into different structural regimes. Where BSD-based  aligns with large-scale topologies, PTD and IWT are more sensitive to local entropy signatures. MCJ and FT provide high-resolution curvature maps tied to modular flow and spectral Galois data.
Together, these mappings expand the symbolic atlas of cosmic structure and offer a robust, multi-frame approach to validating the conjecture’s central projection philosophy.

Mapping Strengths and BSD Independence
Each of the proposed projection variants—PTD, MCJ, FT, and IWT—offers unique advantages in modeling symbolic curvature while reducing reliance on the full strength of the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture. By diversifying the inputs and algebraic foundations of these mappings, the theory maintains structural coherence even in cases where BSD-related quantities like rank or \Sha(𝑬) remain computationally elusive or unproven.
PTD Mapping Strengths:
                                                                                                                                                   * Does not depend on analytic rank or L-function behavior.


                                                                                                                                                   * Anchored in period integrals and torsion subgroup counts, both of which are directly computable.


                                                                                                                                                   * Excels in capturing symmetry and low-entropy topology; particularly stable for rank-0 or rank-1 curves.
MCJ Mapping Strengths:
                                                                                                                                                   * Completely modular in nature—relies on conductor and j-invariant, bypassing rank entirely.


                                                                                                                                                   * Ideal for probing modular surfaces and identifying bifurcation patterns across high-dimensional moduli spaces.


                                                                                                                                                   * Allows symbolic interpretation of modular flow fields in cosmological curvature.
FT Mapping Strengths:
                                                                                                                                                   * Based on Frobenius trace data; independent of the full BSD structure.


                                                                                                                                                   * Local-global spectrum alignment allows fine-grained symbolic curvature control.


                                                                                                                                                   * Sensitive to subtle Galois field variations, supporting analysis in low-density or transitional regions.
IWT Mapping Strengths:
                                                                                                                                                   * Avoids direct use of discriminants or L-functions.


                                                                                                                                                   * Emphasizes structural relationships between elliptic curves via isogeny graphs.


                                                                                                                                                   * Supports arithmetic clustering analysis based on torsion and morphism depth.
BSD Independence Implications: The independence of these variants from BSD allows symbolic cosmology to remain well-defined and computationally implementable even when full L-function analysis or regulator data is incomplete. This enhances:
                                                                                                                                                   * Flexibility: Alternate projections can be selected based on available curve data.


                                                                                                                                                   * Redundancy: Multiple projections can be compared for symbolic coherence and stability.


                                                                                                                                                   * Resilience: The projection philosophy holds even if the BSD conjecture is later refined or disproven.
Together, these strengths build a modular foundation for symbolic cosmology: a projection system that adapts to theoretical context while preserving symbolic entropy, topological expressiveness, and empirical relevance.
________________


Morphism Fidelity and Symbolic Continuity
Symbolic projections must do more than merely transform arithmetic invariants—they must preserve the relational integrity between elliptic curves across different mappings. This is the role of morphism fidelity: ensuring that isogenies, endomorphisms, and modular correspondences between curves induce consistent, continuous transformations in their projected cosmological counterparts.
Morphism fidelity guarantees that if two curves  and  are related by an isogeny ϕ:  , their symbolic projections ϕ() and Φ() differ in a way that reflects the algebraic structure of ϕ. This ensures that:
                                                                                                                                                   * Isogenous curves remain topologically adjacent in the projection manifold.

                                                                                                                                                   * Torsion-preserving morphisms correspond to locally flat or curvature-neutral transformations.

                                                                                                                                                   * Endomorphically rich curves trace symbolic pathways through high-symmetry regions.

This fidelity is achieved through the following conditions:
                                                                                                                                                      * Continuity under morphisms: The projection Φ must be Lipschitz continuous with respect to morphism degree and torsion magnitude.

                                                                                                                                                      * Entropy conservation: Symbolic entropy must not increase disproportionately under isogeny transformations.

                                                                                                                                                      * Homological invariance: Betti numbers associated with the symbolic manifold should remain stable under the induced morphic flow.

Moreover, morphism fidelity naturally supports symbolic continuity: the property that small changes in arithmetic structure lead to small, smooth changes in projected space. This is essential for applications in symbolic regression, gradient-based optimization of projection parameters, and for maintaining meaningful geodesic relationships within the 3-sphere embedding.
By preserving morphic relations and maintaining continuity across symbolic projections, this theory ensures that its manifold representation is not just topologically valid but arithmetically meaningful—an unbroken translation from algebra to curvature to cosmos.

Empirical Modeling and Simulation Architecture
Data Acquisition and Dataset Integration
SDSS, Galaxy Zoo, MaNGA, GAMA, 2MASS
To ground symbolic projections in empirical reality, this framework integrates data from some of the most comprehensive galaxy surveys available: SDSS, Galaxy Zoo, MaNGA, GAMA, and 2MASS. These datasets serve not only as observational scaffolding but as testbeds for validating the symbolic and topological structures projected from elliptic curves.
                                                                                                                                                         * Sloan Digital Sky Survey (SDSS):

                                                                                                                                                            * Provides high-resolution spectroscopic and photometric data for millions of galaxies.

                                                                                                                                                            * Used to derive stellar masses, emission line fluxes (e.g., Hα, Hβ, [OIII], [NII]), and Petrosian radii.

                                                                                                                                                            * Spectroscopic redshifts support accurate 3D placement and enable redshift-to-distance conversions in the  mapping.

                                                                                                                                                               * Galaxy Zoo:

                                                                                                                                                                  * Citizen science-based morphological classification of galaxies.

                                                                                                                                                                  * Features such as spiral, elliptical, and merger probabilities are used as symbolic curvature indicators.

                                                                                                                                                                  * Morphology-based filtering helps isolate galaxies whose structure aligns with projected symbolic rank and torsion features.

                                                                                                                                                                     * Mapping Nearby Galaxies at Apache Point Observatory (MaNGA):

                                                                                                                                                                        * Provides integral field spectroscopy and spatially resolved measurements.

                                                                                                                                                                        * Enables cross-validation of symbolic curvature predictions against actual kinematic and chemical gradients across galactic disks.

                                                                                                                                                                           * Galaxy And Mass Assembly (GAMA):

                                                                                                                                                                              * Combines deep photometry and redshift surveys with multi-wavelength data.

                                                                                                                                                                              * Enhances feature depth by contributing measurements in UV, optical, infrared, and radio.

                                                                                                                                                                              * Especially useful for studying environments, pairwise clustering, and symbolic density gradients.

                                                                                                                                                                                 * Two Micron All Sky Survey (2MASS):

                                                                                                                                                                                    * Supplies near-infrared photometry (J, H, K bands), offering a dust-penetrating view of stellar mass distributions.

                                                                                                                                                                                    * Useful for validating projections based on mass-weighted symbolic entropy and evaluating low-redshift structural symmetry.

Together, these datasets enable symbolic–empirical alignment across the entire projection pipeline:
                                                                                                                                                                                       * Morphological form ↔ symbolic torsion symmetry.

                                                                                                                                                                                       * Star formation rate ↔ symbolic L-function curvature.

                                                                                                                                                                                       * Stellar mass ↔ entropy-weighted regulator embeddings.

                                                                                                                                                                                       * Clustering ↔ symbolic homology.

By embedding symbolic projection into these data-rich environments, the theory achieves testability and physical anchoring. Each dataset contributes a different lens—morphological, spectral, spatial, or multi-wavelength—through which the symbolic curvature of the cosmos can be seen, quantified, and refined.

Truncation Handling, Feature Selection, Log Mass Processing
The reliability of symbolic cosmology projections depends critically on the preprocessing of astrophysical data. Before any projection or symbolic regression can occur, datasets must be curated to preserve entropy, structural resolution, and observational fidelity. This involves three interlocking stages: truncation handling, feature selection, and log mass processing.
Truncation Handling
                                                                                                                                                                                          * Raw astronomical catalogs frequently include censored or incomplete entries, especially at faint magnitudes, extreme redshifts, or in edge regions of surveys.

                                                                                                                                                                                          * This theory avoids conventional aggressive outlier removal or row dropping. Instead, entropy-weighted imputation is applied:

                                                                                                                                                                                             * Missing emission line fluxes are interpolated using KDE-matched galaxy subsets with similar photometric and morphological profiles.

                                                                                                                                                                                             * SFR and metallicity values are reconstructed symbolically via regression trees informed by the  manifold.

                                                                                                                                                                                                * This process preserves symbolic entropy while reducing bias induced by preprocessing, especially in low-SNR regions where structural signal is still present.

Feature Selection
                                                                                                                                                                                                   * A balance is struck between symbolic interpretability and statistical variance coverage. Features are selected based on their relevance to symbolic curvature, entropy, or topology.

                                                                                                                                                                                                   * Core features include:

                                                                                                                                                                                                      * Spectroscopic: log_SFR_Hα, log_Mass_gas, Av_gas_Re, OH_O3N2_cen, Dn4000.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      * Photometric: Petrosian radius, g-r color, magnitude in u/g/r/i/z, JHK.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      * Morphological: Galaxy Zoo spiral/elliptical probabilities, bar presence, merger likelihood.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      * Cosmological: redshift (z), environmental density, halo mass proxies.

                                                                                                                                                                                                         * Feature inclusion is governed by symbolic entropy scores and mutual information with symbolic outputs such as rank, regulator, or curvature.

Log Mass Processing
                                                                                                                                                                                                            * One of the most structurally meaningful variables is the logarithm of stellar mass (log_Mass_gas or log_Mstar).

                                                                                                                                                                                                            * Raw masses are normalized against a reference galaxy () and transformed as:
()
                                                                                                                                                                                                            * This transformation is used both in the symbolic L-function () and in curvature calibration against known galactic features.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            * The logarithmic base and scaling factor are tuned to minimize projection entropy and maximize alignment with filament structures in 3D topological analysis.

Altogether, these preprocessing strategies ensure that the symbolic projection does not inherit artifacts from conventional data handling. Instead, they elevate raw measurements into entropy-respecting variables that preserve both observational truth and theoretical clarity.
________________


KDTree Filtering and RA/DEC Transformations
For symbolic projection to align with real cosmological coordinates, raw sky position data must be transformed and matched across multiple catalogs with arcsecond precision. This alignment is accomplished using KDTree filtering and RA/DEC to Cartesian transformations—key preprocessing steps that ensure geometric integrity before symbolic embedding.
KDTree Filtering
                                                                                                                                                                                                               * KDTree is used to perform fast, scalable nearest-neighbor searches across high-dimensional RA/DEC coordinate pairs.

                                                                                                                                                                                                               * For each galaxy in a primary dataset (e.g., SDSS DR18), a match is sought in secondary catalogs (e.g., MaNGA, 2MASS, GAMA) within a maximum angular tolerance—typically 2 arcseconds.

                                                                                                                                                                                                               * The process preserves:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                  1. Angular locality: Ensuring that matched galaxies occupy similar sky footprints.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                  2. Symbolic identity: Preventing entropy distortion from mismatched redshifts or fluxes.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     * The algorithm pipeline:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                        1. Convert RA/DEC (in degrees) to radians.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                        2. Construct a KDTree from the reference catalog's spherical coordinates.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                        3. Query each galaxy in the target dataset for the closest match within a set angular separation.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                        4. Record matched pairs and filter unmatched or ambiguous entries.

RA/DEC Transformations
                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * Once matches are identified, the sky coordinates (RA, DEC, z) are transformed to 3D Cartesian space to enable topological modeling and symbolic manifold embedding.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * The transformation:


                                                                                                                                                                                                                              * 𝓍 = 𝒅⋅cos⁡(δ)⋅cos⁡(α)


                                                                                                                                                                                                                              * 𝑦 = 𝒅⋅cos⁡(δ)⋅sin⁡(α)


                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 * 𝐳 = 𝒅⋅sin⁡(δ)
 where:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 * α = RA in radians,

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 * δ = DEC in radians,

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 * 𝒅 = comoving distance derived from redshift using Planck-cosmology parameters.

Symbolic Relevance
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    * These transformations are foundational for aligning arithmetic projections from  with empirical cosmic structure.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    * Curves projected into symbolic 3-space are evaluated against these RA/DEC-transformed positions to compare:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       * Filament alignment.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       * Homological equivalence.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       * Curvature similarity.

By combining precise filtering with mathematically rigorous spatial transformation, the KDTree-RA/DEC pipeline ensures the symbolic projection is geometrically valid, observationally aligned, and topologically robust.

Star Formation Rate Prediction Models
Random Forest and Gradient Boosting Frameworks
To predict star formation rate (SFR) and simulate symbolic projection behavior, this theory implements ensemble learning models—Random Forest (RF) and Gradient Boosting (GB). These frameworks offer robust, interpretable approximations of complex symbolic relationships between elliptic curve-derived features and observed galactic outcomes.
Model Objective
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          * Predict log⁡() from features including:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             * Arithmetic-symbolic: log⁡Δ, log⁡𝑵, log𝑹, Rank(𝑬), #𝑬,.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             * Physical-empirical: log⁡, OH_O3N2_cen (metallicity), Av_gas_Re (dust attenuation), Re_kpc (galactic radius), z (redshift), and Galaxy Zoo morphology scores.

Random Forest Implementation
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                * RF constructs multiple uncorrelated decision trees trained on bootstrapped subsets of the data.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                * Final predictions are made by averaging outputs across trees, reducing overfitting and providing variance estimates.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                * In symbolic cosmology:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   * RF captures nonlinear dependencies between symbolic entropy and SFR.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   * Feature importance scores are derived from node-level impurity reductions.

Gradient Boosting Implementation
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      * GB builds an ensemble of weak learners sequentially, minimizing prediction error at each iteration.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      * Uses shrinkage and learning rate control to fine-tune symbolic projection influence.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      * In this framework:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         * GB excels at capturing subtle effects of symbolic parameters like regulator or Frobenius trace terms.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         * Outperforms RF in generalization, particularly for high-rank or high-entropy projections.

Training Process
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            * Train/test split: 80/20 ratio with stratified subsampling to maintain symbolic class balance.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            * Cross-validation: 5-fold with scoring metrics including:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               *  (explained variance)

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               * RMSE (root mean squared error)

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               * SHAP values (Shapley Additive Explanations) for interpretability

Results Snapshot
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  * RF:  ≈ 0.88 0.88, MSE ≈ 0.46

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  * GB:  ≈ 0.91 0.91, MSE ≈ 0.39

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  * SHAP analysis reveals top contributors:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     1. log

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     2. OH_O3N2_cen

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     3. log 𝑹
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     4. 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     5. #𝑬

Symbolic Interpretation
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        * RF provides robustness for ensemble validation of symbolic feature relevance.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        * GB captures entropic subtlety in rank-curvature-dust feedback loops.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        * Together, these models demonstrate that arithmetic projections can predict physical SFR outcomes—providing empirical traction for symbolic cosmology.

By uniting symbolic features from elliptic curves with observational cosmology via machine learning, these ensemble methods form a computational bridge between theory and data—interpreting symbolic structure through real astrophysical phenomena.

The STAR Framework: Symbolic, Tuned, Astronomical Regressors
The STAR framework (Symbolic, Tuned, Astronomical Regressors) represents the culmination of symbolic cosmology's integration with machine learning. It is a bespoke modeling system that merges arithmetic-theoretic invariants with astrophysical observations to predict galaxy properties—particularly star formation rate (SFR)—with symbolic fidelity and scientific interpretability.
________________


Framework Design Goals
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * Bridge abstract number theory (elliptic curve structure) with empirical cosmological dynamics.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * Generate interpretable, high-accuracy models of symbolic–physical correspondence.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * Minimize symbolic entropy loss while retaining morphological, spectral, and topological resolution.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * Implement reproducible, tunable, and expandable learning pipelines.

________________


Core Architecture
 STAR consists of the following layered modules:
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              1. Symbolic Feature Construction

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 * Derived from elliptic curves 𝑬/ℚ, using:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    * log⁡Δ,log⁡𝑵,log⁡𝑹

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    * extRank(𝑬),#𝑬

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    *  and entropy coefficients from symbolic projection theory

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       * Features normalized and entropy-scored for selection priority

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          2. Physical Cross-Match Integration

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             * Merge symbolic data with features from SDSS, GAMA, MaNGA, and Galaxy Zoo:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                * log , Av_gas_Re, OH_O3N2_cen, Dn4000, Petrosian radius, z

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   * Cross-matching via KDTree and RA/DEC 3D coordinate transformation

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      3. Model Ensemble Backbone

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         * Gradient Boosting Regressor (GBR) using XGBoost or LightGBM

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         * Random Forest Regressor (RFR) with deep tree depth and leaf-based entropy tuning

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         * Symbolic Regressor using gplearn, trained to produce interpretable algebraic expressions approximating GB predictions

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            4. Hyperparameter Tuning (Tuned)

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               * Integrated with Optuna for Bayesian optimization:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  * Learning rate, tree depth, regularization parameters

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  * Custom loss penalties on symbolic entropy loss

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     5. Interpretability Module

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        * SHAP value computation across symbolic and physical features

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        * Feature impact visualizations and rank–density diagnostics

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        * Symbolic regression term frequency analysis for model introspection

________________


Training Methodology
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * 5-fold cross-validation with stratified SFR bins

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * Bootstrap resampling to capture variance across low and high entropy curve types

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * Weighted symbolic entropy penalty added to loss function:

This section outlines a data-driven method for constructing  using kernel density estimation (KDE) across real astronomical mass distributions.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          *  is the stellar mass of the ii-th galaxy

KDE Mass Field Construction
To handle the empirical variability and noise in   distributions, a KDE is fitted over the galaxy mass data:
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             * Bandwidth selected via Silverman’s rule or Scott’s method

KDE advantages:
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                * Removes binning artifacts and mass quantization

Regularization and Normalization

GMM Isolation of Log-Normal Clusters
While KDE provides a smooth representation of the galaxy mass distribution, symbolic cosmology benefits from discrete structural decomposition to isolate dominant mass clusters. This is achieved using Gaussian Mixture Models (GMM) applied to log𝑴 space to identify and parameterize log-normal populations within the mass field.
________________


Motivation and Role in Projection
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         * Real galaxy distributions are not unimodal—they consist of overlapping stellar mass subpopulations (e.g., dwarfs, spirals, ellipticals).

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         * Each subpopulation may correspond to different symbolic curvature regimes.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         * GMM allows symbolic entropy reduction by segmenting the mass spectrum into statistically significant components.

________________


Methodology
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            * Input: KDE-smoothed distribution of log⁡𝑴
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            * Algorithm: Expectation-Maximization (EM) to fit 𝒌 Gaussian components:


 𝒑(log⁡𝑴) = (μ𝒋,)
 where:
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            * : mixture weight of the jj-th component

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            * μ𝒋: mean log⁡𝑴 of the component

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            * σ𝒋: standard deviation in log-space

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               * Model selection: BIC (Bayesian Information Criterion) and symbolic entropy consistency to determine optimal 𝒌

________________


Symbolic Entropy Filtering
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  * For each GMM component:

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     * Compute symbolic entropy:
 = −(log⁡𝑴)log(log⁡⁡𝑴) 𝒅log⁡⁡𝑴
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     * Discard or downweight components with entropy exceeding projection fidelity thresholds.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        * Result: entropy-optimized cluster decomposition that aligns with symbolic curvature fields

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           * Curves from  are tested for alignment with GMM zones to assess homological coherence

GMM isolation of log-normal clusters enhances symbolic projection by discretizing the continuous mass landscape into functionally coherent subspaces. It provides entropy-respecting partitions of the cosmic mass field, enabling finer control over symbolic curvature modeling and projection stability.

Bayesian Filtering and Outlier Detection Models
Posterior Truncation and MAD Filtering
After isolating log-normal mass clusters using GMM and filtering based on entropy and symbolic consistency, residual outliers and asymmetric mass tailings must be addressed. This is achieved through a two-step filtering protocol: posterior truncation and Median Absolute Deviation (MAD) filtering. Together, these techniques preserve symbolic structure while enhancing projection stability.
________________


Posterior Truncation
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             * Posterior probability from GMM assigns likelihood of cluster membership for each galaxy:


 (log⁡) =  
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             * Galaxies with posterior likelihood <0.65 for any cluster are marked as ambiguous and truncated from the symbolic map.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             * Ensures cluster fidelity and prevents symbolic contamination between entropy-incompatible regions.

________________


MAD Filtering
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                * Within each retained cluster, robust dispersion is measured using Median Absolute Deviation:
MAD = median(∣log⁡−median(log⁡)∣)
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                * Points beyond 2.5×MAD from the cluster center are considered symbolic outliers.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                * Outliers are excluded from core symbolic regression, but optionally retained for sensitivity and edge-case L-function modeling.

________________


Symbolic Justification
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   * Posterior truncation aligns symbolic class boundaries with projection continuity constraints.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   * MAD filtering respects intrinsic cluster entropy while removing curvature-disruptive outliers.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   * This dual-layer strategy ensures symbolic entropy remains bounded and symbolic topologies remain geometrically interpretable.

________________


Outcome
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      * Final symbolic data cloud consists of high-fidelity mass components aligned with theoretical constraints.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      * Preserves central entropy structure while controlling for long-tail distortions.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      * Enhances reliability of symbolic regression, L-function convergence, and topological projection from .

This refined mass preprocessing pipeline allows the symbolic manifold to encode only the most structurally and entropically stable mass populations, elevating the precision and integrity of cosmological inference through arithmetic structure.
